1f:["$","p",null,{"children":["Put a robot in a corridor with three identical doors and ask it where it is. It has a door\ndetector, and the detector says: ",["$","em",null,{"children":"door"}],"."]}]
20:["$","p",null,{"children":["Now what? A robot that insists on a single answer must pick one of the three doors, and it will\nbe wrong two times out of three. Worse, it will be wrong ",["$","em",null,{"children":"confidently"}],", and every computation\ndownstream — the path it plans, the obstacle it swerves around — inherits that false certainty\nwithout any record that a guess was ever made."]}]
21:["$","p",null,{"children":["The probabilistic answer is to refuse the question as posed. The robot does not know where it\nis; it knows something weaker and far more useful, which is how plausible each location is given\neverything that has happened. That object is a ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"belief"}],", and this chapter is about how to keep\none up to date."]}]
22:["$","$L64",null,{"children":["$","$L65",null,{}]}]
23:["$","p",null,{"children":["The two numbers that decide everything here are draggable. The wheels deliver the commanded step\nplus an error of about ",["$","$L66",null,{"id":"ch05.motionNoise","value":0.12,"min":0.01,"max":0.5,"step":0.01,"unit":"m","role":"prediction","label":"motion noise sigma"}]," — drag that up and watch every peak in\nthe belief widen. The door detector is right about\n",["$","$L66",null,{"id":"ch05.sensorNoise","value":0.18,"min":0.02,"max":0.45,"step":0.01,"role":"measurement","label":"sensor noise"}]," of the time; drag ",["$","em",null,{"children":"that"}]," and watch how much a single sighting is allowed\nto sharpen the belief. Nothing else about the chapter changes — you are turning the two knobs the\nmathematics below will name."]}]
24:["$","p",null,{"children":"Watch it for a few cycles before reading on. Three things are worth noticing, and each one\ncorresponds to a piece of mathematics later in the chapter."}]
25:["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":"The belief is multi-modal, and that is a feature."}]," After the first door sighting there are\nthree peaks. No single number could express \"I am at one of these three places, equally\", and any\nrepresentation that forces a single number — as the Kalman filter of ",["$","$L67",null,{"href":"/chapters/ch06-kalman-filters","children":"Chapter 6"}],"\ndoes — cannot survive this corridor."]}]
26:["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":"Sensing multiplies; moving convolves."}]," The green dashed curve is the measurement likelihood.\nCorrection multiplies the belief by it pointwise, which is why peaks get sharper and mass\nconcentrates. Prediction convolves with the motion noise, which is why everything spreads. Those\ntwo operations, and their opposite effects on certainty, are the whole filter."]}]
27:["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":"Ambiguity dies from sequence, not from evidence."}]," No individual reading distinguishes door one\nfrom door three. What distinguishes them is the ",["$","em",null,{"children":"pattern"}],": door, then a long gap, then a door.\nThe recursion is what accumulates that pattern, and it is the reason a filter beats any single\nmeasurement no matter how good the sensor."]}]
